class="c-article-section__figure-description" data-test="bottom-caption" id="figure-8-desc"><p>Exploring the relationship between model performance and seven types of perturbations.</p></div></div></figure></div><p>The experiments conducted on the standard clean testing set reveal the effective classification capabilities of the seven deep learning models compared in this study. Notably, both PointNet++ and PointMLP achieve the highest overall accuracy, with an OA of 99.7%. In contrast, the OA of the PointCNN model, while relatively lower compared to other models, still reaches a commendable level of 95.2%.</p><p>To further examine the robustness of the models in practical applications, this study evaluates various models on the corruption testing set. This test set simulates common data quality degradation scenarios encountered in real-world settings, including noise, missing data, and jitter, etc. The experimental results demonstrate significant differences in the adaptability of the seven models to different data perturbations, with no single model exhibiting superior performance across all corruption scenarios. Notably, the CurveNet model exhibits the strongest robustness, achieving an OA of 88.5% and a mCE of 0.63.</p><p>In practical agricultural applications, the focus is specifically on five scenarios: <span class="mathjax-tex">\({\rm{Ad}}{{\rm{d}}}_{{\rm{G}}}\)</span>, <span class="mathjax-tex">\({\rm{Ad}}{{\rm{d}}}_{{\rm{L}}}\)</span>, <span class="mathjax-tex">\({\rm{Dro}}{{\rm{p}}}_{{\rm{G}}}\)</span>, <span class="mathjax-tex">\({\rm{Dro}}{{\rm{p}}}_{{\rm{L}}}\)</span>, and Jitter. These scenarios represent situations commonly encountered in acquiring plant point clouds in agricultural settings. The results indicate that models like DGCNN, PCT, and CurveNet, which emphasize global features in non-hierarchical architectures, perform better in terms of overall robustness. The detailed OA parameters are shown in Table <a data-track="click" data-track-label="link" data-track-action="table anchor" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0#Tab5">5</a>. The level indicating the intensity of corruption, where deeper blue signifies lower OA.</p><div class="c-article-table" data-test="inline-table" data-container-section="table" id="table-5"><figure><figcaption class="c-article-table__figcaption"><b id="Tab5" data-test="table-caption">Table 5 Performance comparison of various models in terms of OA under clean and different corruption.</b></figcaption><div class="u-text-right u-hide-print"><a class="c-article__pill-button" data-test="table-link" data-track="click" data-track-action="view table" data-track-label="button" rel="nofollow" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0/tables/5" aria-label="Full size table 5"><span>Full size table</span><svg width="16" height="16" focusable="false" role="img" aria-hidden="true" class="u-icon"><use xmlns:xlink="http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink" xlink:href="#icon-eds-i-chevron-right-small"></use></svg></a></div></figure></div><h3 class="c-article__sub-heading" id="Sec21">Segmentation</h3><p>Point cloud segmentation is a fundamental task in the field of computer vision, encompassing both semantic and instance segmentation. Semantic segmentation aiming to partition point cloud data into subsets with semantic information, while instance segmentation focuses on distinguishing distinct objects or instances within the same category. In the agricultural domain, particularly in the processing of crop point clouds, this task becomes especially vital as it provides foundational support for numerous modern agricultural applications. To gain a deeper understanding of the feasibility of point cloud segmentation in the agricultural domain, nine widely used point cloud segmentation models were selected: PointNet, PointNet++, DGCNN, PointCNN, PointMLP, CurveNet, Stratified Transformer (ST)<sup><a data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" data-test="citation-ref" aria-label="Reference 37" title="Lai, X. et al. Stratified transformer for 3d point cloud segmentation. In: Proceedings of the IEEE/CVF Conference on Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (CVPR). PP 8500–8509, &#xA;                  https://doi.org/10.1109/CVPR52688.2022.00831&#xA;                  &#xA;                 (2022)." href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0#ref-CR37" id="ref-link-section-d399225442e3948">37</a></sup>, PointMamba, and DeLA. For instance segmentation, the current state-of-the-art models on the S3DIS dataset, Mask3D<sup><a data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" data-test="citation-ref" aria-label="Reference 38" title="Schult, J. et al. Mask3D: Mask Transformer for 3D Semantic Instance Segmentation. In: Proceedings of the IEEE International Conference on Robotics and Automation (ICRA). PP 8216–8223, &#xA;                  https://doi.org/10.1109/ICRA48891.2023.10160590&#xA;                  &#xA;                 (2023)." href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0#ref-CR38" id="ref-link-section-d399225442e3952">38</a></sup> and OneFormer3D<sup><a data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" data-test="citation-ref" aria-label="Reference 39" title="Kolodiazhnyi, M., Vorontsova, A., Konushin, A. &amp; Rukhovich, D.OneFormer3D: One Transformer for Unified Point Cloud Segmentation. In: Proceedings of the IEEE/CVF Conference on Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (CVPR). PP 20943–20953, &#xA;                  https://doi.org/10.1109/CVPR52733.2024.01979&#xA;                  &#xA;                 (2024)." href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0#ref-CR39" id="ref-link-section-d399225442e3956">39</a></sup>, were chosen. Detailed testing and evaluation were conducted on the Crops3D dataset. The aim was to evaluate the usability and investigate segmentation models that are better suited for crop point cloud segmentation.</p><h4 class="c-article__sub-heading c-article__sub-heading--small" id="Sec22">Evaluating indicator</h4><p>To comprehensively assess the performance of these models in crop point cloud organ segmentation tasks, this study employs two common semantic segmentation evaluation metrics: Acc and Intersection over Union (IoU). Acc provides an intuitive measure reflecting the model’s ability to correctly segment individual points, while IoU measures the degree of overlap between the model’s segmentation results and the ground truth labels, providing a more robust measure of segmentation accuracy. For evaluating instance segmentation, the Average Precision (AP) metric is used. AP measures the precision of the model’s ability to accurately identify and delineate individual instances across varying levels of detection confidence. The specific formulas are as follows:</p><div id="Equ3" class="c-article-equation"><div class="c-article-equation__content"><span class="mathjax-tex">$${\rm{Acc}}=\frac{{\rm{TP}}+{\rm{TN}}}{{\rm{TP}}+{\rm{TN}}+{\rm{FP}}+{\rm{FN}}}$$</span></div><div class="c-article-equation__number">
                    (3)
                </div></div><div id="Equ4" class="c-article-equation"><div class="c-article-equation__content"><span class="mathjax-tex">$${\rm{IoU}}=\frac{{\rm{TP}}}{{\rm{TP}}+{\rm{FP}}+{\rm{FN}}}$$</span></div><div class="c-article-equation__number">
                    (4)
                </div></div><div id="Equ5" class="c-article-equation"><div class="c-article-equation__content"><span class="mathjax-tex">$${\rm{AP}}={\int }_{0}^{1}p(r){dr}$$</span></div><div class="c-article-equation__number">
                    (5)
                </div></div><p>In this context, True Positives (TP), True Negatives (TN), False Positives (FP), and False Negatives (FN) are the four fundamental elements constituting the confusion matrix in machine learning, which are used to evaluate the performance of models. In calculating AP, two key metrics are precision (<i>p</i>) and recall (<i>r</i>).</p><p>By averaging the Acc, IoU and AP of each organ category, mAcc, mIoU, and mAP can be obtained. These metrics reflect the comprehensive segmentation performance at the scale of the entire crop. The specific formulas are as follows:</p><div id="Equ6" class="c-article-equation"><div class="c-article-equation__content"><span class="mathjax-tex">$${\rm{mAcc}}=\frac{1}{N}{\sum }_{i=1}^{N}{{\rm{Acc}}}_{i}$$</span></div><div class="c-article-equation__number">
                    (6)
                </div></div><div id="Equ7" class="c-article-equation"><div class="c-article-equation__content"><span class="mathjax-tex">$${\rm{mIoU}}=\frac{1}{N}{\sum }_{i=1}^{N}{{\rm{IoU}}}_{i}$$</span></div><div class="c-article-equation__number">
                    (7)
                </div></div><div id="Equ8" class="c-article-equation"><div class="c-article-equation__content"><span class="mathjax-tex">$${\rm{mAP}}=\frac{1}{N}{\sum }_{i=1}^{N}{{\rm{AP}}}_{i}$$</span></div><div class="c-article-equation__number">
                    (8)
                </div></div><p>In this context, <i>N</i> represents the total number of categories in the plant point cloud, where <i>i</i> denotes the <i>i</i>-th organ category (1 ≤ i ≤ N).</p><h4 class="c-article__sub-heading c-article__sub-heading--small" id="Sec23">Model comparison</h4><p>Moving on to the results, Tables <a data-track="click" data-track-label="link" data-track-action="table anchor" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0#Tab6">6</a>, <a data-track="click" data-track-label="link" data-track-action="table anchor" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0#Tab7">7</a> present the comparative results of nine widely recognized models for the task of semantic segmentation on the Crops3D dataset. The mAcc per crop, as shown in Table <a data-track="click" data-track-label="link" data-track-action="table anchor" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0#Tab6">6</a>, indicates that all models achieve high segmentation accuracy. This can be attributed to the fact that leaf points constitute a significant portion of the crop point clouds, leading to a severe class imbalance problem. Consequently, models can attain high accuracy by correctly identifying the majority of leaf points. In contrast, mIoU serves as a more reliable metric for assessing the precision of segmentation. Table <a data-track="click" data-track-label="link" data-track-action="table anchor" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0#Tab7">7</a> reveals that the segmentation of complex crops, such as maize and tomato, still poses significant challenges. Furthermore, as the number of crop segmentation categories increases, all models exhibit a notable decline in mIoU performance.</p><div class="c-article-table" data-test="inline-table" data-container-section="table" id="table-6"><figure><figcaption class="c-article-table__figcaption"><b id="Tab6" data-test="table-caption">Table 6 Acc performance of various methods in the segmentation of eight crop species.</b></figcaption><div class="u-text-right u-hide-print"><a class="c-article__pill-button" data-test="table-link" data-track="click" data-track-action="view table" data-track-label="button" rel="nofollow" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0/tables/6" aria-label="Full size table 6"><span>Full size table</span><svg width="16" height="16" focusable="false" role="img" aria-hidden="true" class="u-icon"><use xmlns:xlink="http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink" xlink:href="#icon-eds-i-chevron-right-small"></use></svg></a></div></figure></div><div class="c-article-table" data-test="inline-table" data-container-section="table" id="table-7"><figure><figcaption class="c-article-table__figcaption"><b id="Tab7" data-test="table-caption">Table 7 IoU performance of various methods in the segmentation of eight crop species.</b></figcaption><div class="u-text-right u-hide-print"><a class="c-article__pill-button" data-test="table-link" data-track="click" data-track-action="view table" data-track-label="button" rel="nofollow" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0/tables/7" aria-label="Full size table 7"><span>Full size table</span><svg width="16" height="16" focusable="false" role="img" aria-hidden="true" class="u-icon"><use xmlns:xlink="http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink" xlink:href="#icon-eds-i-chevron-right-small"></use></svg></a></div></figure></div><p>Plants were subdivided into seven parts, including Soil, Stem or Branch, Leaves, Fruits, Tassel, Pot, and Others. The detailed results can be found in Table <a data-track="click" data-track-label="link" data-track-action="table anchor" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0#Tab8">8</a> and Table <a data-track="click" data-track-label="link" data-track-action="table anchor" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0#Tab9">9</a>, numerical values represent IoU scores of the segmentation results, with deeper blue indicating lower IoU and deeper red indicating higher IoU. When evaluating the performance of the models, from the perspective of OA, all models seem to perform well in segmenting crop point clouds. However, a common issue arises when dealing with crop point clouds: the proportion of leaves is often relatively high, making it challenging to assess the segmentation quality based solely on OA. Therefore, we primarily consider IoU as the performance evaluation metric, as shown in Table <a data-track="click" data-track-label="link" data-track-action="table anchor" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0#Tab7">7</a>.</p><div class="c-article-table" data-test="inline-table" data-container-section="table" id="table-8"><figure><figcaption class="c-article-table__figcaption"><b id="Tab8" data-test="table-caption">Table 8 Segmentation performance of classical methods on the Crop3D dataset for eight crop species.</b></figcaption><div class="u-text-right u-hide-print"><a class="c-article__pill-button" data-test="table-link" data-track="click" data-track-action="view table" data-track-label="button" rel="nofollow" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0/tables/8" aria-label="Full size table 8"><span>Full size table</span><svg width="16" height="16" focusable="false" role="img" aria-hidden="true" class="u-icon"><use xmlns:xlink="http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink" xlink:href="#icon-eds-i-chevron-right-small"></use></svg></a></div></figure></div><div class="c-article-table" data-test="inline-table" data-container-section="table" id="table-9"><figure><figcaption class="c-article-table__figcaption"><b id="Tab9" data-test="table-caption">Table 9 Segmentation performance of recent methods on the Crop3D dataset for eight crop species.</b></figcaption><div class="u-text-right u-hide-print"><a class="c-article__pill-button" data-test="table-link" data-track="click" data-track-action="view table" data-track-label="button" rel="nofollow" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0/tables/9" aria-label="Full size table 9"><span>Full size table</span><svg width="16" height="16" focusable="false" role="img" aria-hidden="true" class="u-icon"><use xmlns:xlink="http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink" xlink:href="#icon-eds-i-chevron-right-small"></use></svg></a></div></figure></div><p>In the task of instance segmentation within plots, the categories were divided into ‘Others’ and ‘Plant’, with detailed results presented in Table <a data-track="click" data-track-label="link" data-track-action="table anchor" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0#Tab10">10</a>. The OneFormer3D and Mask3D models achieve an Average Precision (AP) of over 0.96 for the ‘Others’ background category, demonstrating high segmentation performance for background. However, challenges remain in accurately segmenting overlapping and occluded plant parts within plots. The overall AP dropped to 0.80, which implies a marked decline in performance at higher IoU thresholds (e.g., 0.75 or above). This indicates that while the models perform adequately for coarse alignment, their precision in capturing finer details is insufficient, leading to segmentation errors in intricate areas.</p><div class="c-article-table" data-test="inline-table" data-container-section="table" id="table-10"><figure><figcaption class="c-article-table__figcaption"><b id="Tab10" data-test="table-caption">Table 10 Detailed instance segmentation performance on the Crops3D_IS dataset.</b></figcaption><div class="u-text-right u-hide-print"><a class="c-article__pill-button" data-test="table-link" data-track="click" data-track-action="view table" data-track-label="button" rel="nofollow" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0/tables/10" aria-label="Full size table 10"><span>Full size table</span><svg width="16" height="16" focusable="false" role="img" aria-hidden="true" class="u-icon"><use xmlns:xlink="http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink" xlink:href="#icon-eds-i-chevron-right-small"></use></svg></a></div></figure></div><p>The visualization results are illustrated in Fig. <a data-track="click" data-track-label="link" data-track-action="figure anchor" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0#Fig9">9</a>, with Fig. <a data-track="click" data-track-label="link" data-track-action="figure anchor" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0#Fig9">9a</a> showing the semantic segmentation results and Fig. <a data-track="click" data-track-label="link" data-track-action="figure anchor" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0#Fig9">9b</a> displaying the instance segmentation results. The circled areas indicate discrepancies with the ground truth. The experiments indicate that the segmentation performance of the nine models is notably good for Rice and Wheat. This is because these crops are annotated into broader categories such as ‘Shoot system’ and ‘Others’, without requiring segmentation of detailed internal plant structures. However, for Maize, which includes numerous fine-grained subcategories and exhibits intricate intersections between different organs, the models show poorer segmentation performance, particularly in the transition zones between different plant parts. Notably, in the Fruits category, as one of the most critical components of the plant, all models face challenges in segmentation. As depicted in Fig. <a data-track="click" data-track-label="link" data-track-action="figure anchor" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0#Fig9">9a</a>, the three crops, Cotton, Maize, and Tomato, with the Fruits category, significant instances of oversegmentation occur in Cotton and Tomato. In instance segmentation task, models generally perform well for widely spaced plants. However, in densely planted conditions, such as with Maize, the models struggle with accurately segmenting overlapping leaves, as depicted in Fig. <a data-track="click" data-track-label="link" data-track-action="figure anchor" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0#Fig9">9b</a>. This indicates the necessity for improvements in handling complex and densely packed vegetation structures. Additionally, the models show an incomplete understanding of plant structures, often resulting in fragmented instances in the segmentation results.</p><div class="c-article-section__figure js-c-reading-companion-figures-item" data-test="figure" data-container-section="figure" id="figure-9" data-title="Fig. 9"><figure><figcaption><b id="Fig9" class="c-article-section__figure-caption" data-test="figure-caption-text">Fig. 9</b></figcaption><div class="c-article-section__figure-content"><div class="c-article-section__figure-item"><picture class="c-article-section__figure-picture"><source type="image/webp" srcset="//media.springernature.com/lw685/springer-static/image/art%3A10.1038%2Fs41597-024-04290-0/MediaObjects/41597_2024_4290_Fig9_HTML.png?as=webp"><img aria-describedby="figure-9-desc" src="//media.springernature.com/lw685/springer-static/image/art%3A10.1038%2Fs41597-024-04290-0/MediaObjects/41597_2024_4290_Fig9_HTML.png" alt="Fig. 9" loading="lazy" width="685" height="990"></picture><div class="c-article-section__figure-link"><a class="c-article__pill-button" data-test="article-link" data-track="click" data-track-label="button" data-track-action="view figure" href="/articles/s41597-024-04290-0/figures/9" data-track-dest="link:Figure9 Full size image" aria-label="Full size image figure 9" rel="nofollow"><span>Full size image</span><svg width="16" height="16" focusable="false" role="img" aria-hidden="true" class="u-icon"><use xmlns:xlink="http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink" xlink:href="#icon-eds-i-chevron-right-small"></use></svg></a></div></div><div class="c-article-section__figure-description" data-test="bottom-caption" id="figure-9-desc"><p>Segmentation results of multiple models in the Crops3D dataset.</p></div></div></figure></div></div></div></section>
                        </div>
                    

                    <div class="u-mt-32">
                        <section data-title="Code availability"><div class="c-article-section" id="code-availability-section"><h2 class="c-article-section__title js-section-title js-c-reading-companion-sections-item" id="code-availability">Code availability</h2><div class="c-article-section__content" id="code-availability-content">
              
              <p>The programming language utilized in this work is Python 3.6.13. The associated python environment, including all necessary dependencies, is documented in the requirements (txt) and env (yaml) files. These files ensure that the environment can be accurately replicated for reproducibility purposes. Additionally, the subsampling, corruption scripts, conversion to S3DIS format scripts, along with other code-related content, are available through the following GitHub repository: <a href="https://github.com/clawCa/Crops3D">https://github.com/clawCa/Crops3D</a>.</p>
